Quick patches mask underlying issues.\n\n**Core principle:** ALWAYS find root cause before attempting fixes. Symptom fixes are failure.\n\n**Violating the letter of this process is violating the spirit of debugging.**\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO FIXES WITHOUT ROOT CAUSE INVESTIGATION FIRST\n```\n\nIf you haven't completed Phase 1, you cannot propose fixes.\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse for ANY technical issue:\n- Test failures\n- Bugs in production\n- Unexpected behavior\n- Performance problems\n- Build failures\n- Integration issues\n\n**Use this ESPECIALLY when:**\n- Under time pressure (emergencies make guessing tempting)\n- \"Just one quick fix\" seems obvious\n- You've already tried multiple fixes\n- Previous fix didn't work\n- You don't fully understand the issue\n\n**Don't skip when:**\n- Issue seems simple (simple bugs have root causes too)\n- You're in a hurry (rushing guarantees rework)\n- Manager wants it fixed NOW (systematic is faster than thrashing)\n\n## The Four Phases\n\nYou MUST complete each phase before proceeding to the next.\n\n### Phase 1: Root Cause Investigation\n\n**BEFORE attempting ANY fix:**\n\n1. **Read Error Messages Carefully**\n   - Can you trigger it reliably?\n   - What are the exact steps?\n   - Does it happen every time?\n   - If not reproducible → gather more data, don't guess\n\n3. **Check Recent Changes**\n   - What changed that could cause this?\n   - Git diff, recent commits\n   - New dependencies, config changes\n   - Environmental differences\n\n4. **Gather Evidence in Multi-Component Systems**\n\n   **WHEN system has multiple components (CI → build → signing, API → service → database):**\n\n   **BEFORE proposing fixes, add diagnostic instrumentation:**\n   ```\n   For EACH component boundary:\n     - Log what data enters component\n     - Log what data exits component\n     - Verify environment/config propagation\n     - Check state at each layer\n\n   Run once to gather evidence showing WHERE it breaks\n   THEN analyze evidence to identify failing component\n   THEN investigate that specific component\n   ```\n\n   **Example (multi-layer system):**\n   ```bash\n   # Layer 1: Workflow\n   echo \"=== Secrets available in workflow: ===\"\n   echo \"IDENTITY: ${IDENTITY:+SET}${IDENTITY:-UNSET}\"\n\n   # Layer 2: Build script\n   echo \"=== Env vars in build script: ===\"\n   env | grep IDENTITY || echo \"IDENTITY not in environment\"\n\n   # Layer 3: Signing script\n   echo \"=== Keychain state: ===\"\n   security list-keychains\n   security find-identity -v\n\n   # Layer 4: Actual signing\n   codesign --sign \"$IDENTITY\" --verbose=4 \"$APP\"\n   ```\n\n   **This reveals:** Which layer fails (secrets → workflow ✓, workflow → build ✗)\n\n5. **Trace Data Flow**\n\n   **WHEN error is deep in call stack:**\n\n   See `root-cause-tracing.md` in this directory for the complete backward tracing technique.\n\n   **Quick version:**\n   - Where does bad value originate?\n   - What called this with bad value?\n   - Keep tracing up until you find the source\n   - Fix at source, not at symptom\n\n### Phase 2: Pattern Analysis\n\n**Find the pattern before fixing:**\n\n1. **Find Working Examples**\n   - Locate similar working code in same codebase\n   - What works that's similar to what's broken?\n\n2. **Compare Against References**\n   - If implementing pattern, read reference implementation COMPLETELY\n   - Don't skim - read every line\n   - Understand the pattern fully before applying\n\n3. **Identify Differences**\n   - What's different between working and broken?\n   - List every difference, however small\n   - Don't assume \"that can't matter\"\n\n4. **Understand Dependencies**\n   - What other components does this need?\n   - What settings, config, environment?\n   - What assumptions does it make?\n\n### Phase 3: Hypothesis and Testing\n\n**Scientific method:**\n\n1. **Form Single Hypothesis**\n   - State clearly: \"I think X is the root cause because Y\"\n   - Write it down\n   - Be specific, not vague\n\n2. **Test Minimally**\n   - Make the SMALLEST possible change to test hypothesis\n   - One variable at a time\n   - Don't fix multiple things at once\n\n3. **Verify Before Continuing**\n   - Did it work? Yes → Phase 4\n   - Didn't work? Form NEW hypothesis\n   - DON'T add more fixes on top\n\n4. **When You Don't Know**\n   - Say \"I don't understand X\"\n   - Don't pretend to know\n   - Ask for help\n   - Research more\n\n### Phase 4: Implementation\n\n**Fix the root cause, not the symptom:**\n\n1. **Create Failing Test Case**\n   - Simplest possible reproduction\n   - Automated test if possible\n   - One-off test script if no framework\n   - MUST have before fixing\n   - Use the `ultrapowers:test-driven-development` skill for writing proper failing tests\n\n2. **Implement Single Fix**\n   - Address the root cause identified\n   - ONE change at a time\n   - No \"while I'm here\" improvements\n   - No bundled refactoring\n\n3. **Verify Fix**\n   - Test passes now?\n   - No other tests broken?\n   - Issue actually resolved?\n   - If the bug was meaningful or systemic, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` before declaring the fix complete.\n\n4. **If Fix Doesn't Work**\n   - STOP\n   - Count: How many fixes have you tried?\n   - If < 3: Return to Phase 1, re-analyze with new information\n   - **If ≥ 3: STOP and question the architecture (step 5 below)**\n   - DON'T attempt Fix #4 without architectural discussion\n\n5. **If 3+ Fixes Failed: Question Architecture**\n\n   **Pattern indicating architectural problem:**\n   - Each fix reveals new shared state/coupling/problem in different place\n   - Fixes require \"massive refactoring\" to implement\n   - Each fix creates new symptoms elsewhere\n\n   **STOP and question fundamentals:**\n   - Is this pattern fundamentally sound?\n   - Are we \"sticking with it through sheer inertia\"?\n   - Should we refactor architecture vs. continue fixing symptoms?\n\n   **Discuss with your human partner before attempting more fixes**\n\n   This is NOT a failed hypothesis - this is a wrong architecture.\n\n## Red Flags - STOP and Follow Process\n\nIf you catch yourself thinking:\n- \"Quick fix for now, investigate later\"\n- \"Just try changing X and see if it works\"\n- \"Add multiple changes, run tests\"\n- \"Skip the test, I'll manually verify\"\n- \"It's probably X, let me fix that\"\n- \"I don't fully understand but this might work\"\n- \"Pattern says X but I'll adapt it differently\"\n- \"Here are the main problems: [lists fixes without investigation]\"\n- Proposing solutions before tracing data flow\n- **\"One more fix attempt\" (when already tried 2+)**\n- **Each fix reveals new problem in different place**\n\n**ALL of these mean: STOP. Return to Phase 1.**\n\n**If 3+ fixes failed:** Question the architecture (see Phase 4.5)\n\n## your human partner's Signals You're Doing It Wrong\n\n**Watch for these redirections:**\n- \"Is that not happening?\" - You assumed without verifying\n- \"Will it show us...?\" - You should have added evidence gathering\n- \"Stop guessing\" - You're proposing fixes without understanding\n- \"Ultrathink this\" - Question fundamentals, not just symptoms\n- \"We're stuck?\" (frustrated) - Your approach isn't working\n\n**When you see these:** STOP. Return to Phase 1.\n\n## Common Rationalizations\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Issue is simple, don't need process\" | Simple issues have root causes too. Process is fast for simple bugs. |\n| \"Emergency, no time for process\" | Systematic debugging is FASTER than guess-and-check thrashing. |\n| \"Just try this first, then investigate\" | First fix sets the pattern. Do it right from the start. |\n| \"I'll write test after confirming fix works\" | Untested fixes don't stick. Test first proves it. |\n| \"Multiple fixes at once saves time\" | Can't isolate what worked. Causes new bugs. |\n| \"Reference too long, I'll adapt the pattern\" | Partial understanding guarantees bugs. Read it completely. |\n| \"I see the problem, let me fix it\" | Seeing symptoms ≠ understanding root cause. |\n| \"One more fix attempt\" (after 2+ failures) | 3+ failures = architectural problem. Question pattern, don't fix again. |\n\n## Quick Reference\n\n| Phase | Key Activities | Success Criteria |\n|-------|---------------|------------------|\n| **1. Root Cause** | Read errors, reproduce, check changes, gather evidence | Understand WHAT and WHY |\n| **2. Pattern** | Find working examples, compare | Identify differences |\n| **3. Hypothesis** | Form theory, test minimally | Confirmed or new hypothesis |\n| **4. Implementation** | Create test, fix, verify | Bug resolved, tests pass |\n\n## When Process Reveals \"No Root Cause\"\n\nIf systematic investigation reveals issue is truly environmental, timing-dependent, or external:\n\n1. You've completed the process\n2. Document what you investigated\n3. Implement appropriate handling (retry, timeout, error message)\n4. Components must subscribe to the smallest slice they need, preferably with selector hooks and equality checks, so unrelated UI does not re-render.\n- Never let app shells, page roots, sidebars, timelines, or other broad containers subscribe to an entire high-churn Manager snapshot when they only need stable fields. Draft text, cursors, hover/open state, search text, and other per-keystroke or per-pointer state need narrow component-level subscriptions.\n- If typing in an input, moving a cursor, toggling a hover/open flag, or editing a small draft makes the whole app repaint, the subscription boundary is wrong even if the data source is centralized.\n- Always implement a custom data manager for any app data structure.\n- Never use separate state variables or arrays as the source of truth.\n- Never send data around in transactions. Pass intent, ids, commands, or Manager method calls instead.\n\n## React Control Flow\n\n- Avoid using `useEffect()` to update state. Prefer `useCallback()` handlers or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.\n- Work ahead of time to avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.\n- When debugging React and a fix does not work, explicitly consider whether the root cause is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.\n- Every async callback, mutation result, timer, poll, or stream message that can update visible state must prove it still owns the current context before mutating Managers or navigation.\n- Use `RefObject` instead of `MutableRefObject`.\n\n## Routing And Modules\n\n- Prefer explicit routing over file-based routing when the framework does not strongly default to file-based routes. Framework-native file routing, such as Next.js App Router, is fine.\n- Never use `require()` to import modules. Use `import` instead.\n- Avoid `npm` by default. Use `pnpm` for package installation, scripts, and lockfiles unless the user or existing project explicitly chooses another package manager.\n\n## Next.js And SSR Defaults\n\n- Treat `\"use client\"` as a boundary with a cost. Do not put an entire page, app shell, sidebar, timeline, composer, settings surface, and data boot path behind one giant client component unless there is a deliberate reason.\n- SSR is only useful if the initial HTML contains meaningful above-the-fold UI or data. If `curl` for the route only shows a boot shell, script payloads, or empty placeholders, the route is not meaningfully server-rendered even if SSR is technically enabled.\n- Server-render the first useful screen by fetching route-stable bootstrap data on the server: auth/session state, settings needed to choose the shell, selected route ids, and initial page/snapshot data. Hydrate Managers or query caches from that bootstrap data; do not wait for client `useEffect()` plus client fetches before the first meaningful paint.\n- Use client effects for browser-only concerns: localStorage-only preferences, notifications, SSE/WebSocket subscriptions, focus management, and post-paint refreshes. Do not use effects as the only path for data required to decide what the initial screen is.\n- In Next dev, first request time includes compilation. Distinguish cold compile cost from runtime slowness before optimizing the wrong layer.\n\n## Import And Bundle Hygiene\n\n- Keep imports proportional to the UI being rendered. Do not import an entire complex surface for a tiny control; split shared tiny controls into their own files instead of importing them from heavy feature modules.\n- Lazy-load rarely opened or route-conditional surfaces with dynamic imports: settings dialogs, side panels, file viewers, terminals, worker inspectors, onboarding flows, QR/pairing dialogs, and large markdown/code viewers.\n- Avoid package and barrel imports that drag in whole icon packs, design systems, syntax highlighters, markdown stacks, charting libraries, or component suites for one glyph or one primitive. Inline small SVG paths or use local assets when that is cheaper and stable.\n- Prefer native browser controls for small, ordinary selects and pickers. Do not import a popover/dialog/menu stack for a low-value dropdown unless search, virtualization, or rich item rendering is genuinely needed.\n- Use subpath imports when a package supports them. Avoid root package imports from large UI libraries if a narrower entrypoint exists.\n- After dependency or UI-shell changes, inspect the emitted dev chunks or traces. Verify whether heavy modules moved out of the root chunk rather than assuming dynamic import worked.\n\n## Performance Debugging\n\n- Start with evidence: dev server logs, route timings, emitted chunk sizes, module counts, and initial HTML content.\n- Separate long-lived requests from slow requests. SSE, streaming, and hanging fetches can show huge durations in logs without blocking page paint.\n- Check recent dependency additions first when compile time suddenly explodes. A single icon, markdown, chart, or UI package can add thousands of modules.\n- For Next.js root-load complaints, answer three questions before changing code: what compiled, what HTML was returned, and what client-side requests gated the first useful paint?\n\n## Frontend Configuration\n\n- Never store frontend string literals or UI settings in `.env` files.\n- Put frontend strings and settings in normal source files, inline arrays, or `.json` files so they remain reviewable, testable, and versioned with the UI.\n\n## Internationalization\n\n- For meaningful React UI work, offer language support as an option when the user has not already decided: \"Should we support other languages? If yes, I will wire user-facing copy through i18n resources now instead of hardcoding strings.\"\n- If the user says yes, asks for translation/i18n/language/locale support, or reports that language switching only updates part of the UI, use `implementing-react-i18n`.\n- Use the i18n library's `t(\"key\", params?)` function for every user-facing string. Use `useI18nSnapshot()` only to subscribe React components so language changes repaint.\n- Never name variables with locale codes or locale-like names such as `it`, `en`, `enUS`, `locIt`, or `locEnUs`.\n\n## Settings UI\n\n- For settings dialogs, preferences panels, configuration modals, option tabs, or UI settings surfaces, use `designing-settings-dialogs`.\n\n"
  },

    "type": "input_text",
    "text": "FILE /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/test-driven-development/SKILL.md\n---\nname: test-driven-development\ndescription: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n---\n\n# Test-Driven Development (TDD)\n\n## Overview\n\nWrite the test first. Watch it fail. Write minimal code to pass.\n\n**Core principle:** If you didn't watch the test fail, you don't know if it tests the right thing.\n\n**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**\n\n## When to Use\n\n**Always:**\n- New features\n- Bug fixes\n- Refactoring\n- Behavior changes\n\n**Exceptions (ask your human partner):**\n- Throwaway prototypes\n- Generated code\n- Configuration files\n\n**Do directly with targeted verification:**\n- tiny color, spacing, copy, or label changes,\n- one-line prompt wording changes,\n- docs-only wording changes,\n- mechanical edits where the expected result is obvious from the diff.\n\nDo not perform a full red-green cycle for a tiny mechanical edit unless it changes behavior or there is a real regression risk. For example, changing a button from gray to blue usually needs the edit plus a targeted visual or code check, not a new failing test.\n\nThinking \"skip TDD just this once\"? Stop. That's rationalization.\n\n## UI-Specific Note\n\nWhen the task is UI work, choose the design system and starting block before writing tests around the intended behavior and rendering. In Ultrapowers, that means `shadcn/ui` plus the closest available ShadCN Block for each new screen, form, or major surface unless the user says otherwise. Inspect https://ui.shadcn.com/blocks first, install or copy the selected block, and then adapt it. Before adding any individual control, check whether `shadcn/ui` already provides a suitable control and use/customize that control first.\n\nFor important app workflows, also consider whether `agentic-user-journey-testing` should be proposed. Do not run that higher-cost black-box test mode without explicit user approval.\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST\n```\n\nWrite code before the test? Delete it. Start over.\n\n**No exceptions:**\n- Don't keep it as \"reference\"\n- Don't \"adapt\" it while writing tests\n- Don't look at it\n- Delete means delete\n\nImplement fresh from tests. Period.\n\n## Red-Green-Refactor\n\n```dot\ndigraph tdd_cycle {\n    rankdir=LR;\n    red [label=\"RED\\nWrite failing test\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ffcccc\"];\n    verify_red [label=\"Verify fails\\ncorrectly\", shape=diamond];\n    green [label=\"GREEN\\nMinimal code\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ccffcc\"];\n    verify_green [label=\"Verify passes\\nAll green\", shape=diamond];\n    refactor [label=\"REFACTOR\\nClean up\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ccccff\"];\n    next [label=\"Next\", shape=ellipse];\n\n    red -> verify_red;\n    verify_red -> green [label=\"yes\"];\n    verify_red -> red [label=\"wrong\\nfailure\"];\n    green -> verify_green;\n    verify_green -> refactor [label=\"yes\"];\n    verify_green -> green [label=\"no\"];\n    refactor -> verify_green [label=\"stay\\ngreen\"];\n    verify_green -> next;\n    next -> red;\n}\n```\n\n### RED - Write Failing Test\n\nWrite one minimal test showing what should happen.\n\n<Good>\n```typescript\ntest('retries failed operations 3 times', async () => {\n  let attempts = 0;\n  const operation = () => {\n    attempts++;\n    if (attempts < 3) throw new Error('fail');\n    return 'success';\n  };\n\n  const result = await retryOperation(operation);\n\n  expect(result).toBe('success');\n  expect(attempts).toBe(3);\n});\n```\nClear name, tests real behavior, one thing\n</Good>\n\n<Bad>\n```typescript\ntest('retry works', async () => {\n  const mock = jest.fn()\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\n    .mockResolvedValueOnce('success');\n  await retryOperation(mock);\n  expect(mock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n});\n```\nVague name, tests mock not code\n</Bad>\n\n**Requirements:**\n- One behavior\n- Clear name\n- Real code (no mocks unless unavoidable)\n- Real product behavior is the target. Mocked behavior can never be the final deliverable or proof that the feature works.\n\n### Verify RED - Watch It Fail\n\n**MANDATORY. Never skip.**\n\n```bash\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\n```\n\nConfirm:\n- Test fails (not errors)\n- Failure message is expected\n- Fails because feature missing (not typos)\n\n**Test passes?** You're testing existing behavior. Fix test.\n\n**Test errors?** Fix error, re-run until it fails correctly.\n\n### GREEN - Minimal Code\n\nWrite simplest code to pass the test.\n\n<Good>\n```typescript\nasync function retryOperation<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n    try {\n      return await fn();\n    } catch (e) {\n      if (i === 2) throw e;\n    }\n  }\n  throw new Error('unreachable');\n}\n```\nJust enough to pass\n</Good>\n\n<Bad>\n```typescript\nasync function retryOperation<T>(\n  fn: () => Promise<T>,\n  options?: {\n    maxRetries?: number;\n    backoff?: 'linear' | 'exponential';\n    onRetry?: (attempt: number) => void;\n  }\n): Promise<T> {\n  // YAGNI\n}\n```\nOver-engineered\n</Bad>\n\nDon't add features, refactor other code, or \"improve\" beyond the test.\n\n### Verify GREEN - Watch It Pass\n\n**MANDATORY.**\n\n```bash\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\n```\n\nConfirm:\n- Test passes\n- Other tests still pass\n- Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\n\n**Test fails?** Fix code, not test.\n\n**Other tests fail?** Fix now.\n\n### REFACTOR - Clean Up\n\nAfter green only:\n- Remove duplication\n- Improve names\n- Extract helpers\n\nKeep tests green. Don't add behavior.\n\n### Repeat\n\nNext failing test for next feature.\n\n## Good Tests\n\n| Quality | Good | Bad |\n|---------|------|-----|\n| **Minimal** | One thing. \"and\" in name? Split it. | `test('validates email and domain and whitespace')` |\n| **Clear** | Name describes behavior | `test('test1')` |\n| **Shows intent** | Demonstrates desired API | Obscures what code should do |\n\n## Why Order Matters\n\n**\"I'll write tests after to verify it works\"**\n\nTests written after code pass immediately. Passing immediately proves nothing:\n- Might test wrong thing\n- Might test implementation, not behavior\n- Might miss edge cases you forgot\n- You never saw it catch the bug\n\nTest-first forces you to see the test fail, proving it actually tests something.\n\n**\"I already manually tested all the edge cases\"**\n\nManual testing is ad-hoc. You think you tested everything but:\n- No record of what you tested\n- Can't re-run when code changes\n- Easy to forget cases under pressure\n- \"It worked when I tried it\" ≠ comprehensive\n\nAutomated tests are systematic. They run the same way every time.\n\n**\"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful\"**\n\nSunk cost fallacy. The time is already gone. Your choice now:\n- Delete and rewrite with TDD (X more hours, high confidence)\n- Keep it and add tests after (30 min, low confidence, likely bugs)\n\nThe \"waste\" is keeping code you can't trust. Working code without real tests is technical debt.\n\n**\"TDD is dogmatic, being pragmatic means adapting\"**\n\nTDD IS pragmatic:\n- Finds bugs before commit (faster than debugging after)\n- Prevents regressions (tests catch breaks immediately)\n- Documents behavior (tests show how to use code)\n- Enables refactoring (change freely, tests catch breaks)\n\n\"Pragmatic\" shortcuts = debugging in production = slower.\n\n**\"Tests after achieve the same goals - it's spirit not ritual\"**\n\nNo. Tests-after answer \"What does this do?\" Tests-first answer \"What should this do?\"\n\nTests-after are biased by your implementation. You test what you built, not what's required. You verify remembered edge cases, not discovered ones.\n\nTests-first force edge case discovery before implementing. Tests-after verify you remembered everything (you didn't).\n\n30 minutes of tests after ≠ TDD. You get coverage, lose proof tests work.\n\n## Common Rationalizations\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Too simple to test\" | Simple code breaks. Test takes 30 seconds. |\n| \"I'll test after\" | Tests passing immediately prove nothing. |\n| \"Tests after achieve same goals\" | Tests-after = \"what does this do?\" Tests-first = \"what should this do?\" |\n| \"Already manually tested\" | Ad-hoc ≠ systematic. No record, can't re-run. |\n| \"Deleting X hours is wasteful\" | Sunk cost fallacy. Keeping unverified code is technical debt. |\n| \"Keep as reference, write tests first\" | You'll adapt it. That's testing after. Delete means delete. |\n| \"Need to explore first\" | Fine. Throw away exploration, start with TDD. |\n| \"Test hard = design unclear\" | Listen to test. Hard to test = hard to use. |\n| \"TDD will slow me down\" | TDD faster than debugging. Pragmatic = test-first. |\n| \"Manual test faster\" | Manual doesn't prove edge cases. You'll re-test every change. |\n| \"Existing code has no tests\" | You're improving it. Add tests for existing code. |\n\n## Red Flags - STOP and Start Over\n\n- Code before test\n- Test after implementation\n- Test passes immediately\n- Can't explain why test failed\n- Tests added \"later\"\n- Rationalizing \"just this once\"\n- \"I already manually tested it\"\n- \"Tests after achieve the same purpose\"\n- \"It's about spirit not ritual\"\n- \"Keep as reference\" or \"adapt existing code\"\n- \"Already spent X hours, deleting is wasteful\"\n- \"TDD is dogmatic, I'm being pragmatic\"\n- \"This is different because...\"\n\n**All of these mean: Delete code. Start over with TDD.**\n\n## Example: Bug Fix\n\n**Bug:** Empty email accepted\n\n**RED**\n```typescript\ntest('rejects empty email', async () => {\n  const result = await submitForm({ email: '' });\n  expect(result.error).toBe('Email required');\n});\n```\n\n**Verify RED**\n```bash\n$ npm test\nFAIL: expected 'Email required', got undefined\n\n"
  }

Use dependency injection. |\n| Test setup huge | Extract helpers. Still complex? Simplify design. |\n\n## Debugging Integration\n\nBug found? Write failing test reproducing it. Follow TDD cycle. Test proves fix and prevents regression.\n\nNever fix bugs without a test.\n\n## Testing Anti-Patterns\n\nWhen adding mocks or test utilities, read @testing-anti-patterns.md to avoid common pitfalls:\n- Testing mock behavior instead of real behavior\n- Adding test-only methods to production classes\n- Mocking without understanding dependencies\n\n## Final Rule\n\n```\nProduction code → test exists and failed first\nOtherwise → not TDD\n```\n\nNo exceptions without your human partner's permission.\n\n"

    "type": "input_text",
    "text": "FILE /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/client-server-state-invariants/SKILL.md\n      86 /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/client-server-state-invariants/SKILL.md\n---\nname: client-server-state-invariants\ndescription: Use when planning, building, reviewing, or debugging React apps with server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, persistence, or client/server synchronization\n---\n\n# Client Server State Invariants\n\nReact bugs often expose deeper ownership mistakes. When client state, server state, cache state, stream state, and persisted state all exist, every plan needs explicit invariants.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nNo UI surface may treat data as authoritative unless the payload proves ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and terminal state.\n\nIf those words are missing from the plan, the plan is probably baking in a race condition.\n\n## Planning Checklist\n\nFor every React app that reads server/runtime state, the plan must answer:\n\n1. **Owner:** What owns this state: server, client manager, URL, cache, stream, local storage, or worker/runtime?\n2. **Token:** What owner token protects async results: `requestId`, `runId`, `projectPath`, `operationId`, `workerId + seq`, or equivalent?\n3. **Provenance:** Is data server-authoritative, optimistic, cached preview, partial update, or historical fallback?\n4. **Completeness:** Does the payload declare what scope it is complete for? What may it erase?\n5. **Ordering:** What stable order is used: monotonic seq, `(createdAt, id)`, version, or cursor?\n6. **State machine:** What are the legal states, terminal states, and transitions?\n7. **Events:** Which server decisions are named events rather than client-side derivations?\n8. **Loading shape:** Which states are bootstrap, first load, incremental refresh, reconnect, resync, recovery, and terminal error?\n9. **Fallbacks:** Which legacy/fallback rows can render, and only after what authority is loaded?\n10. **Tests:** Which race, stale-response, event-sequence, reconnect, and merge tests prove the invariants?\n\n## Invariants To Require\n\n- Cached and optimistic data may pre-render, but cannot satisfy full-load gates.\n- Partial snapshots may add or refine state, but may not erase optimistic objects, delivered user input, read markers, or stream entries unless they declare complete scope.\n- Every async response checks that its owner token still matches before mutating visible state.\n- Mutation success handlers may navigate or clear UI only if they still own the current context, unless the action explicitly creates a new selected context.\n- Timers and polling callbacks re-check ownership immediately before acting.\n- Server decisions are emitted as named events; the client does not infer decisions by diffing snapshots.\n- SSE/WebSocket streams have event ids, replay/resume, and an explicit resync path.\n- Loading booleans are split by lifecycle stage. A leaf stream refresh must not unmount the whole page.\n- Append-only content has one writer, one reader, stable ids, and monotonic cursors.\n- Fallback DB rows or historical rows are gated behind stream loading and deduped by stable id plus safe equivalence.\n- Status classifiers distinguish active, runnable, blocked, awaiting user, terminal, stale, and recoverable states.\n- \"Latest\" queries have deterministic tie-breakers. Timestamp-only ordering is not enough.\n- Hot paths use bounded reads and do not synchronously load large bodies, bridge probes, schema setup, or heavy app graphs.\n- Process-local caches have explicit TTL/size bounds and invalidation rules.\n\n## Test Matrix\n\nPick the rows that match the work. Do not rely on one happy-path UI test.\n\n| Risk | Test shape |\n| --- | --- |\n| Stale async response | Start request A, switch owner, resolve A, assert it cannot mutate B |\n| Optimistic object erased | Create local optimistic state, apply stale/partial snapshot, assert object remains |\n| Cache masquerades as loaded | Hydrate cache preview, assert full-load gate waits for server authority |\n| Mutation steals navigation | Start mutation, switch selection, resolve mutation, assert focus stays put |\n| Timer/poll stale-fire | Use fake timers or deferred promises; callback must re-check owner before acting |\n| Snapshot merge erases stream | Apply partial snapshot missing known entries, assert entries/read markers remain |\n| Event stream gap | Bootstrap snapshot, emit events during disconnect, reconnect with last id, assert replay or resync |\n| Server decision invisible | Exercise branch and assert named event such as `blocked`, `refused`, `gave_up`, or `error.surfaced` |\n| State classifier wrong | Table-test every status into active/runnable/terminal/recoverable buckets |\n| Fallback duplicates stream | Load fallback rows then stream entries, assert no duplicate or false chronology |\n| Hot path bloats | Assert snapshots carry cursors not bodies; route returns before DB/heavy imports when possible |\n| Timestamp tie | Insert equal timestamps, assert deterministic order by id/seq |\n\nUse headless HTTP/SSE tests for control-plane decisions. Use React/unit tests for manager merging and ownership guards. Use Playwright only for real UI layout, focus, scroll, mobile computed styles, and visible affordances.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- one `isLoading` boolean for bootstrap, refresh, reconnect, and recovery;\n- cache or optimistic data treated as authoritative without provenance;\n- async callbacks that mutate state without checking owner tokens;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- snapshot diffs used as the only way to know what happened;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- timestamp-only \"latest\" queries;\n- parallel transcript or persistence surfaces that the frontend must reconcile;\n- unbounded process caches introduced to hide slow storage;\n- tests that prove only the DOM result when the bug is a server decision.\n\n## Review Prompt\n\nAsk this before implementation starts:\n\n> What can arrive late, out of order, partially, twice, or after the user changes context, and what test proves it cannot corrupt visible state?\n\n"

    "text": "FILE /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/designing-settings-dialogs/SKILL.md\n      66 /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/designing-settings-dialogs/SKILL.md\n---\nname: designing-settings-dialogs\ndescription: Use when designing, planning, building, or reviewing settings dialogs, settings dialogues, preferences panels, configuration modals, option tabs, or UI settings surfaces\n---\n\n# Designing Settings Dialogs\n\n## Core Principle\n\nA settings dialog is a control surface, not a narrative page. It should help users find, understand, and change preferences with minimal chrome.\n\n## Defaults\n\n- Do not repeat the selected tab, sidebar item, or section name as a heading inside the panel.\n- Do not add subtitles that explain obvious groups.\n- Do not describe what visible controls already say.\n- Prefer compact rows: label, optional current value, control, validation or error when needed.\n- Add helper text only when it explains consequences, scope, persistence, sync, privacy, security, irreversible behavior, constraints, or ambiguous terminology.\n- Keep hierarchy shallow: tabs or sidebar sections at the top level, then grouped controls.\n- Avoid nested cards, decorative wrappers, intro copy, empty headings, and repeated \"Settings\" labels.\n- Use status text only for real state: unsaved changes, saving, saved, failed, unavailable, permission required.\n- Make save semantics explicit: auto-save, Apply/Cancel, or Save. Do not mix them casually.\n- Preserve the host platform or design-system conventions before inventing custom settings chrome.\n\n## Content Test\n\nBefore adding any heading, subtitle, description, icon, badge, or callout, ask:\n\n1. Would the user be confused without this text?\n2. Does it explain a consequence or boundary the control itself cannot show?\n3. Does it change what the user should do?\n\nIf all answers are no, delete it.\n\n## Persistence And Scope\n\nEvery meaningful setting still needs explicit behavior:\n\n- whether it persists,\n- where it is stored,\n- who owns it,\n- when it is loaded and saved,\n- whether it is local, workspace-level, account-level, or synced,\n- how reset, migration, and verification work.\n\nThis persistence design belongs in the plan or spec, not as explanatory prose inside the dialog unless the user needs it to make a safe choice.\n\n## Common Mistakes\n\n| Mistake | Better |\n|---|---|\n| Tab says \"Appearance\", panel title says \"Appearance\", subtitle says \"Customize how the app looks.\" | Use the tab label only. Show the controls. |\n| \"Notification settings let you control notifications.\" | Delete it. |\n| A card per setting with icon, title, subtitle, and repeated copy. | Use dense labeled rows unless the setting has complex consequences. |\n| \"Configure your editor preferences below.\" | Delete it. The dialog already provides the context. |\n| Helper text under every control for visual rhythm. | Add helper text only where it carries decision-critical information. |\n\n## Review Checklist\n\n- One clear navigation model.\n- No repeated tab or section titles.\n- No subtitles for self-explanatory sections.\n- No helper text that merely restates a label.\n- Controls are scannable without decorative cards.\n- Save, cancel, reset, and error behavior are visible when relevant.\n- Persistence and scope are designed, even when not narrated in the UI.\n\n"
  },
  {
    "type": "input_text",
    "text": "FILE /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/instrumenting-control-planes/SKILL.md\n      94 /Users/masterman/.agents/skills/instrumenting-control-planes/SKILL.md\n---\nname: instrumenting-control-planes\ndescription: Use when building, planning, reviewing, or debugging apps with backend decisions, agent workflows, long-running jobs, SSE/WebSocket streams, recovery paths, deletes, retries, or user-visible failures\n---\n\n# Instrumenting Control Planes\n\nBuild the control plane as a product surface. If the server decides, refuses, retries, spawns, deletes, recovers, gives up, or fails, that decision must be observable by users, operators, and tests.\n\nIf a React UI consumes the control plane through server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, or persisted runtime state, also use `client-server-state-invariants`.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nEvery meaningful server-side state transition emits a named, durable event. The UI and tests consume the same production event surface.\n\nDo not rely on snapshot diffs, rendered DOM state, logs only, or client-side re-derivation to prove what the server decided.\n\n## Hot Path Rule\n\nNo user bootstrap, catalog, status, reconnect, or recovery request may depend on a lock-prone database write path or external health probe finishing. Serve these paths from a bounded read model, memory cache, append-only event log, or stale-but-marked snapshot; queue reconciliation writes outside the request. Every dependency on a database or bridge call in a hot path needs a short timeout, cancellation, and an explicit degraded response.\n\nIf SQLite, local files, or a single-writer store is used, assume writer contention can freeze readers at the worst moment. Use one writer, WAL/busy-timeout discipline, short transactions, bounded queues, and headless chaos tests that run bootstrap/reload while writes are active. A 25s UI request is a control-plane failure, even if the database eventually returns.\n\n## Event Contract\n\nFor each backend or agent workflow, define:\n\n- named events for decisions: `worker.spawned`, `worker.reattached`, `plan.ready`, `plan.review.blocked`, `recovery.gave_up`, `conversation.delete_failed`;\n- payloads with stable ids, `prev`/`next` status when relevant, reason codes, and enough context to debug;\n- `error.surfaced` or an equivalent user-facing failure event for every failure the user should know about;\n- durable persistence or a bounded replay buffer so short disconnects do not erase history;\n- a scriptable read path: CLI, HTTP endpoint, sqlite query, or event log command.\n\nIf a decision cannot be asserted from this surface, the design is incomplete.\n\nIf debugging a control-plane failure reveals a reusable lesson, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` after the fix is verified.\n\n## Streaming Requirements\n\nFor SSE or WebSocket-like streams:\n\n- emit named events alongside any snapshot/update frame;\n- include monotonic event ids;\n- support resume from the last seen id;\n- replay missed events from a bounded buffer;\n- emit an explicit resync-required event when replay is impossible;\n- bootstrap from a snapshot, then tail events.\n\nSnapshots show current truth. Named events show why it became true.\n\n## Testing Standard\n\nControl-plane tests should usually be headless HTTP/SSE scenario tests, not browser tests. Browser automation is for UI behavior; control-plane tests assert server decisions directly.\n\nWrite scenario assertions as event transcripts:\n\n```ts\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.spawned\", { runId });\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.status\", { next: \"running\" });\nchaos.killServer();\nchaos.restartServer();\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.reattached\", { runId });\nexpect(events).not.toHaveEmitted(\"worker.recreated\", { runId });\n```\n\nChaos belongs outside production code paths: drop connections in the client, fail fetches in the harness, restart the server process, and use seeds for reproducibility.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- silent early returns in server methods;\n- swallowed `catch` blocks;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- response-code-only user failures;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- tests that infer control-plane behavior from rendered state;\n- delete handlers that do not enumerate dependent rows or emit typed failure events;\n- recovery loops without terminal events such as `gave_up` or `abandoned`;\n- request handlers that wait on inline reconciliation writes, SQLite contention, or bridge `/doctor` checks before returning bootstrap data;\n- test-only behavior branches in production code.\n\n## Design Checklist\n\nBefore implementing a backend or agent workflow, answer:\n\n1. What decisions can this workflow make?\n2. What named event is emitted for each decision, including refusal and failure?\n3. Where are events persisted or buffered?\n4. How does a client resume after disconnect?\n5. Which request paths must keep working while persistence or bridge dependencies are slow, locked, or stale?\n6. How can a CLI or test harness inspect the exact event sequence without the UI?\n7. Which headless scenario proves the happy path, failure path, recovery path, terminal state, and hot-path timeout/degraded path?\n8. Which client state consumes these events, and how does it prove ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and replay/resync?\n\nIf the answer is \"the UI can figure it out\", name the missing event instead.\n\n"
  }

## Codex progress · 2026-08-01T15:22:13.994Z

I’ve found a concrete mismatch in the code: the editor accepts a password and labels the action “Connect,” but some paths deliberately do nothing with that password. I’m checking which path applies here—the current website’s own server versus a separately added server—because both currently lead back to the same old password screen for different reasons.

So the tunnel only needs to expose **one port — 3000**. The browser hits `af.cloudcap.fun` → cloudflared → `127.0.0.1:3000` → Next rewrites `/api` → `127.0.0.1:8000`. Auth cookies (`httponly`, `samesite=lax`) work fine over this same-origin HTTPS path, and CORS never triggers.\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-07-31-android-pwa-stream-reconnect.md:6:**Root Cause:** The web runtime treated every EventSource `error` event as terminal, explicitly closed the stream, and let `RunnerConnection` classify the whole server as offline. Chrome on Android can emit a transient EventSource error while it is automatically reconnecting after a mobile network or PWA lifecycle interruption. Even after preserving that reconnecting stream, Android can restore a page with the native EventSource indefinitely stuck in its reconnecting state; the app had no foreground lifecycle transition that explicitly retried a recoverable connection. Separately, the standalone HTTP adapter reconstructs an internal HTTP request URL, so same-origin password login did not recognize the explicitly configured HTTPS public origin unless the proxy was broadly trusted.\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-07-31-android-pwa-stream-reconnect.md:7:**Fix:** Keep EventSource alive while its `readyState` says it is reconnecting, represent that state as degraded, and restore Online when the stream reopens. Start a bounded fallback retry when native reconnect begins and cancel it if EventSource reopens first. On `visibilitychange` to visible, `pageshow`, or browser `online`, explicitly retry server connections already classified as degraded or offline. Reserve Offline for terminal stream/bootstrap failures and do not retry states that require user action, such as reauthentication. Accept the exact `OMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` as a same-origin login origin without trusting arbitrary forwarded headers.\ndocs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-23-remote-access-pwa-design.md:185:- require same-origin `Origin` or `Referer` validation on authenticated mutating routes,\ndocs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-13-managed-claude-model-gateway-design.md:190:Authentication is required. Mutations enforce same-origin checks. Inputs are schema-validated and unknown fields/actions are rejected. `connect` returns the OAuth URL and state; the UI opens it and retains a visible fallback link. The server owns bounded completion polling and emits the result through the canonical status/event path.\ndocs/codex-history/conversations/2026/06/2026-06-26-inspect-session-spawn-failure-019f055f-31e7-7b52-af6b-24f8dcaac308-part-001.md:2153:{\"id\":\"a46315b0-0ea9-47a4-882c-28f7f4000a03\",\"seq\":1,\"runId\":\"2a9aadf3a768\",\"kind\":\"execution_events\",\"createdAt\":\"2026-06-26T19:05:24.397Z\",\"payload\":{\"summary\":\"Read /Users/masterman/NLP/cloudcaptions/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-26-server-side-transcription-prelayout.md for supervisor context.\",\"path\":\"/Users/masterman/NLP/cloudcaptions/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-26-server-side-transcription-prelayout.md\",\"absolutePath\":\"/Users/masterman/NLP/cloudcaptions/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-26-server-side-transcription-prelayout.md\",\"content\":\"# Server-Side Transcription Prelayout Implementation Plan\\n\\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use ultrapowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or ultrapowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. Do not implement until this plan is approved.\\n\\n**Goal:** When Transcribe finishes, the editor receives a real CloudCap caption layout DTO with the new caption document so the first caption render does not wait on cold browser-side exact layout.\\n\\n**Architecture:** Keep transcription normalization, line redistribution, timing, and layout under the existing CloudCap contracts. The server computes an initial full exact layout for the normalized caption document during the transcription flow, returns it as `{ signature, placedCues }`, and stores it through the same `placedLayoutJson` / local `placedLayout` cache path the editor already trusts. The client accepts server prelayout only when its current layout signature matches, then falls back to the existing browser `layoutPlacedCues` path when absent, stale, unsupported, or failed.\\n\\n**Tech Stack:** Next.js App Router, React 19, TypeScript, Bun, Rust/WASM layout engine, `@napi-rs/canvas` for server/headless canvas, existing caption/timing repositories, existing `placedLayoutJson` cache, Bun tests, Rust/WASM parity tests.\\n\\n**North Star Product:** A creator can upload a video, click Transcribe, and immediately see correctly placed captions as soon as transcription completes, with long transcripts warming server-side instead of blocking the browser's first exact layout.\\n\\n**Current Milestone:** Add server-side initial layout precompute to the direct transcription endpoint and editor adoption path, with parity/benchmark proof and no substitute caption renderer.\\n\\n**Future Product Direction:** Stream ASR block output into a buffered server layout queue when providers expose incremental transcripts; add durable background layout jobs/SSE for long cloud projects; later evaluate native Rust service layout once it proves parity with the JS reference and browser exact path.\\n\\n**Final Functionality Standard:** For cloud and local projects, a successful direct transcription response can include a complete exact layout DTO for the returned caption document. If the DTO signature matches the editor's resolved cues/style, the editor installs it before automatic browser layout starts, skips the initial cold exact pass, and still renders strictly from the normal CloudCap placed-cue DTO. If server layout cannot produce a verified matching DTO, the existing browser path remains the real fallback and the user sees an explicit non-fatal layout-precompute diagnostic in logs/events.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Product And PM Pass\\n\\n- Primary user: the creator editing a newly uploaded video.\\n- Primary job: click Transcribe and start reviewing captions without waiting through the first cold caption layout.\\n- Supporting jobs: preserve local-project browser persistence, preserve cloud track persistence, expose enough status/debugging for failed server prelayout, and keep later style/line edits on the existing client layout path.\\n- Current providers appear response-oriented rather than incremental-streaming in this route. This milestone computes layout immediately after the backend transcript is normalized and redistributed. The implementation should shape the layout service around buffered transcript blocks so a future streaming provider can feed it line batches, but it must not fake streaming behavior for providers that return one final result.\\n- No new UI surface is required. The existing Transcribe button, processing state, caption editor, and preview are reused. If user-visible copy changes, keep it minimal and route it through existing editor copy patterns.\\n- Work in the current repository. Do not create a branch or worktree unless the user explicitly asks.\\n\\n## Hard Constraints\\n\\n- Do not hand-implement subtitle placement, tokenization, packing, cavity scoring, fallback, timing projection, or layout verification in TypeScript/frontend.\\n- Do not introduce `layoutSubtitlePreview` or any substitute user-visible render path.\\n- Server prelayout must produce the same DTO shape consumed by the editor's `layoutResult` path.\\n- The client must verify ownership, freshness, completeness, and ordering before adopting server layout:\\n  - Owner: current editor `editId` and current transcription request id/idempotency key.\\n  - Freshness: returned layout `signature` equals the client-computed `layoutSignature`.\\n  - Completeness: layout covers every non-empty cue required by `hasCompleteExactPlacedLayout`.\\n  - Ordering: result belongs to the latest transcription request; stale responses cannot mutate the current caption document or layout.\\n- Both SQLite/libSQL and PostgreSQL remain supported through existing dialect-neutral repositories.\\n- Raw media bytes remain audio-only through the transcription endpoint. Layout precompute receives text/style/timing DTOs only, not video frames.\\n\\n## Current Findings\\n\\n- `src/app/api/edits/timing-jobs/transcribe/route.ts` returns a normalized `captionDocument`; local projects are stateless, cloud projects use the timing-job control plane.\\n- `src/server/timing-backends/control-plane.ts` already redistributes transcription output via `DEFAULT_TRANSCRIPTION_REDISTRIBUTE_TARGET_WORDS_PER_LINE`.\\n- `src/app/(app)/editor/[editId]/editor-client.tsx` replaces the document after transcription, which changes `layoutSignature` and triggers cold client exact layout.\\n- The editor already restores `initialPlacedLayout` when `{ signature, placedCues }` matches the current signature.\\n- `/api/edits/layout-cache` and `CaptionRepository.savePlacedLayoutByEditId` already persist matching cloud layout DTOs.\\n- Prior spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-19-backend-caption-layout-offload-brainstorm.md` warns that `cloudcaptions_layout_cli` is not obviously equivalent to the current browser exact demand-measurement path. V1 must either run the same app/WASM/headless canvas path server-side or first prove/upgrade CLI parity.\\n- `editor-client.tsx` is 3729 lines. Any implementation should keep edits there minimal and move new logic into small helpers.\\n\\n## State Model And Events\\n\\n- `transcriptionRequestId`: generated client-side per Transcribe click and sent with the request. The client stores the latest id and ignores older responses.\\n- `idempotencyKey`: still owns timing-job idempotency for cloud jobs.\\n- `targetWordsPerLine`: sent explicitly by the client. Default is the current transcription redistribution target. This makes the active split rule explicit even before a user-facing transcription split preference exists.\\n- `precomputedLayout`: response payload:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype ServerPrecomputedLayout = {\\n  signature: string;\\n  placedCues: CaptionPlacedCue[];\\n  source: \\\"server-transcription-prelayout\\\";\\n  rendererVersion: string;\\n  measurementBackend: \\\"headless-app-wasm\\\";\\n  durationMs: number;\\n  cueCount: number;\\n};\\n\\ntype LayoutPrecomputeError = {\\n  code: string;\\n  message: string;\\n};\\n```\\n\\n`CaptionPlacedCue` must come from an existing shared contract type such as `src/lib/caption-render/types.ts`. If the current type is not server-safe, move only the DTO type definition into a shared non-React module during Task 1.\\n\\n- Timing events to add for job-backed cloud flows:\\n  - `caption_layout.precompute.started`\\n  - `caption_layout.precompute.completed`\\n  - `caption_layout.precompute.skipped`\\n  - `caption_layout.precompute.failed`\\n- Stateless local flows cannot write timing-job events. They should return `precomputedLayout` or `layoutPrecomputeError` in the response and log structured server diagnostics.\\n\\n## File Map\\n\\nFiles to create:\\n- `src/lib/caption-layout/signature.ts`: pure shared helpers for `CAPTION_LAYOUT_ENGINE_VERSION`, `serializeLayoutSignature`, `getLayoutRelevantStyle`, and server/client payload validation. This avoids importing client-only editor modules from server code.\\n- `src/server/caption-layout/transcription-prelayout.ts`: server service that converts a `CaptionDocument` plus resolved layout style into legacy cues, builds the exact layout request, runs the server layout runner with timeout/size guards, validates completeness, returns `ServerPrecomputedLayout`.\\n- `src/server/caption-layout/headless-layout-runner.ts`: isolated headless app/WASM layout runner using `@napi-rs/canvas`, registered CloudCap fonts, and the same `layoutPlacedCues(buildEditorCaptionLayoutRequest(...))` path used by the browser. Prefer an isolated module or worker/child process if global `OffscreenCanvas`/`fetch` shims would leak into request handling. Concrete WASM boot strategy: do not depend on an HTTP server for `/wasm/wasm_layout_bg.wasm`; either add a server-safe `initWasmLayoutFromBuffer(wasmBytes: Uint8Array)` wrapper around the generated `initSync`/`init` module or make the isolated runner shim `globalThis.fetch` only inside the worker/child process so that `/wasm/wasm_layout_bg.wasm*` reads `public/wasm/wasm_layout_bg.wasm` from disk. The chosen path must be covered by a test and must not leak a global fetch shim into normal request handling.\\n- `tests/server-transcription-prelayout.test.ts`: unit/integration coverage for server prelayout service, response adoption shape, stale signature rejection, and fallback behavior.\\n- `tests/editor-transcription-prelayout-source.test.ts`: guard that transcription adoption installs matching `precomputedLayout` before automatic layout scheduling and never uses a substitute renderer.\\n- `scripts/benchmark-transcription-prelayout.mjs`: required reproducible benchmark comparing current client-first layout time vs server prelayout time on fixture transcripts.\\n\\nFiles to modify:\\n- `package.json`: no new dependency is expected because `@napi-rs/canvas` is already present in `devDependencies`; verify it is installable/usable in the local Bun runtime and keep the transcription route on the Node.js runtime if necessary. Do not move this route to an edge runtime.\\n- `src/app/api/edits/timing-jobs/transcribe/route.ts`: accept `targetWordsPerLine`, `layoutStyle`, and `transcriptionRequestId`; call prelayout after normalization for both local and cloud responses; return `precomputedLayout` or `layoutPrecomputeError`.\\n- `src/server/timing-backends/control-plane.ts`: parameterize transcription redistribution target, call server prelayout for job-backed direct transcription, persist cloud `placedLayoutJson` when auto-applied or replace-applied, record named prelayout events.\\n- `src/server/timing-backends/events.ts`: add caption layout precompute event names.\\n- `src/server/repositories/caption.ts`: only if needed, add a single dialect-neutral method that saves document and placed layout together. Avoid a second DB update if implementation can safely combine them.\\n- `src/server/repositories/timing-job.ts`: use existing `timing_results.backend_payload_json` for the pending-import prelayout envelope, or add dialect-neutral repository methods if the existing shape needs a safer typed helper. Avoid schema changes unless `backend_payload_json` cannot be used.\\n- `src/lib/editor-caption-pipeline.ts`: expose any pure request-building pieces needed server-side without importing React/client modules. Keep layout/timing ownership unchanged.\\n- `src/app/(app)/editor/[editId]/caption-style-config.tsx`: move `getLayoutRelevantStyle` and the key set into `src/lib/caption-layout/signature.ts`, then re-export for existing UI imports.\\n- `src/app/(app)/editor/[editId]/editor-helpers.ts`: import/re-export `CAPTION_LAYOUT_ENGINE_VERSION` and `serializeLayoutSignature` from the shared helper; keep existing tests green.\\n- `src/app/(app)/editor/[editId]/editor-client.tsx`: send explicit layout style/split metadata with Transcribe; adopt matching `precomputedLayout` with latest-request and signature guards; persist local `placedLayout` after adopting. Keep this edit narrow.\\n- `tests/timing-direct-transcription.test.ts`: add server prelayout event/result cases and target-word redistribution parameter tests.\\n- `tests/timing-control-plane.test.ts`: existing test file; update if control-plane result/event assertions need the new prelayout event names or pending-import payload.\\n- `tests/editor-helpers.test.ts`: adjust imports if signature helpers move.\\n- `tests/editor-layout-prewarm-source.test.ts`: existing source guard; update/add assertions that server prelayout adoption does not introduce substitute renderers and does not call initial exact layout when complete matching layout is installed.\\n- `tests/reference-render-parity.test.ts`: existing parity test; run and extend only if the server runner needs a fixture-specific assertion.\\n- `tests/dom-preview-reference-parity.test.tsx`: existing DOM parity test; run as a correctness gate.\\n- `tests/local-project-stub-editor.test.ts` / `tests/local-project-mirror.test.ts`: update only if local prelayout persistence changes mirrored payload shape.\\n\\nCandidate agentic user journey test, approval required before running:\\n- Mission: log in as `qa@cloudcaptions.dev`, upload a clip, click Transcribe, and verify the first caption appears immediately after transcription without a blocking \\\"Processing captions\\\" cold-layout wait.\\n- Entry point: `http://localhost:4015/login`, then gallery/editor flow.\\n- Visible proof: editor preview shows first caption, subtitle panel contains transcribed lines, network response includes `precomputedLayout`, and no foreground full-document client layout starts before adoption.\\n\\n`.gitignore` coverage:\\n- Do not commit audio/video fixtures, benchmark output, temporary PNGs, server-runner traces, local `.env`, logs, coverage, `node_modules`, or generated one-off artifacts.\\n- If a benchmark fixture is needed, use a tiny text/caption JSON fixture under `tests/fixtures` rather than media bytes.\\n\\n## Implementation Tasks\\n\\n### 1. Baseline And Contract Extraction\\n- [ ] Add failing tests around the desired behavior before implementation:\\n  - `serializeLayoutSignature` remains byte-compatible after moving helpers.\\n  - a matching `precomputedLayout` marks the layout complete without calling initial client exact layout.\\n  - stale `transcriptionRequestId` or stale `signature` is ignored.\\n- [ ] Capture baseline evidence for a representative normalized transcript:\\n  - client cold exact active-cue latency,\\n  - full placed-cue prewarm time,\\n  - first-caption visible delay after current transcription.\\n- [ ] Move `CAPTION_LAYOUT_ENGINE_VERSION`, layout-affecting style keys, `getLayoutRelevantStyle`, and `serializeLayoutSignature` into `src/lib/caption-layout/signature.ts`.\\n- [ ] Update editor imports/re-exports with no behavior change.\\n- Verification: `bun test tests/editor-helpers.test.ts tests/editor-layout-prewarm-source.test.ts`.\\n\\n### 2. ## Codex progress · 2026-08-01T15:25:30.471Z

The cause is now clear. The form reloads the saved password into the field, but the Connect handler only reauthorizes a native app when the typed password is different from that saved value. If the credential is missing or expired and you submit the same correct password, the handler merely closes the dialog—no login request is made—so the app correctly remains on the old password screen. In a browser, editing the current server is even more direct: that authorization function returns without using the password at all.

I’m fixing both no-op paths: Connect will reauthorize native connections even when the password is unchanged, and it will use the submitted password to unlock the current same-origin server.

Future agents should be able to read the note and avoid the same mistake.\n\n## Update The System When The Lesson Generalizes\n\nIf the bug reveals a reusable rule, update the nearest skill or defaults doc in the same change. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?\n2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)\n3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures\n4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?\n   - If NO: State actual status with evidence\n   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence\n5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim\n\nSkip any step = lying, not verifying\n```\n\n## Product-Surface Verification\n\nWhen the work involves an app, UI, or product surface, verification must cover more than whether a single requested feature works.\n\nKeep verification proportional. For tiny mechanical edits, targeted verification is enough: inspect the diff, confirm the changed value/text, and run only the smallest relevant check. Do not escalate to full product-surface verification unless the change can affect behavior, state, navigation, persistence, or the user journey.\n\nAlso verify:\n\n- required functionality is real end-to-end, not a fake component, mock, placeholder, canned response, or fallback path,\n- the primary user stories are actually supported,\n- the primary user journey works end-to-end,\n- any approved agentic user journey tests have been run, or clearly state that they were proposed but not run because approval was not given,\n- return/revisit flows work when they are part of the product,\n- expected empty states are present,\n- expected loading, waiting, or in-progress states are visible,\n- expected error and recovery states are present,\n- expected completed or success states are understandable,\n- expected status-awareness flows are present,\n- the state model is represented correctly,\n- React/server-state invariants are verified when the app uses server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, streams, background jobs, or persisted runtime state,\n- persistence behavior for settings, preferences, drafts, and other user-managed state matches the approved design,\n- operational readiness expectations are met for the approved scope,\n- instrumentation and observability exist where the approved scope depends on them,\n- backend or agent workflow decisions emit the planned named events, and a headless/scriptable check can assert the event sequence without relying on UI snapshots,\n- real errors are surfaced rather than silently swallowed or replaced with vague placeholders,\n- onboarding and discoverability work for first-run or empty-state use,\n- risk and trust surfaces are handled well enough to avoid silent failure, ambiguous state, or obvious loss of confidence,\n- when the scope includes a frontend/backend boundary, frontend-visible failures preserve the real error details and stack traces expected by the approved design,\n- baseline expected v1 surfaces and inferred behaviors from the approved story set, spec, or plan are actually present.\n\nWhen the scope includes settings or preferences, also verify:\n\n- each meaningful setting persists or does not persist exactly as designed,\n- the storage location and ownership model match the approved plan,\n- refresh, restart, sign-out, reset, and migration behavior work as intended for the approved scope,\n- settings dialogs avoid redundant section titles, subtitles, explanatory filler, and decorative chrome that does not help the user make a decision.\n\nDo not confuse \"the requested control exists\" with \"the product is usable.\"\n\nFor app, UI, and product-surface work, consider whether an agentic user journey test is the right proof. This mode is approval-gated because it can spend significant tokens: propose it when useful, but only run it if the user explicitly approves.\n\n## Common Failures\n\n| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |\n|-------|----------|----------------|\n| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, \"should pass\" |\n| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |\n| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |\n| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |\n| Meaningful system bug fixed | Learning note plus verification | Fix committed with no durable lesson |\n| React/server state sync works | Stale-response, merge, event-sequence, and ownership tests relevant to the change | UI looked right once |\n| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |\n| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports \"success\" |\n| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |\n| Feature complete | Real end-to-end behavior in the product | Mocked component, canned data, placeholder UI, fallback path |\n\n## Red Flags - STOP\n\n- Using \"should\", \"probably\", \"seems to\"\n- Expressing satisfaction before verification (\"Great!\", \"Perfect!\", \"Done!\", etc.)\n- About to commit/push/PR without verification\n- Trusting agent success reports\n- Accepting \"validated with mocks\" or \"placeholder works\" as completion\n- Relying on partial verification\n- Thinking \"just this once\"\n- Tired and wanting work over\n- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**\n\n## Rationalization Prevention\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Should work now\" | RUN the verification |\n| \"I'm confident\" | Confidence ≠ evidence |\n| \"Just this once\" | No exceptions |\n| \"Linter passed\" | Linter ≠ compiler |\n| \"Agent said success\" | Verify independently |\n| \"I'm tired\" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |\n| \"Partial check is enough\" | Partial proves nothing |\n| \"Different words so rule doesn't apply\" | Spirit over letter |\n\n## Key Patterns\n\n**Tests:**\n```\n✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] \"All tests pass\"\n❌ \"Should pass now\" / \"Looks correct\"\n```\n\n**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**\n```\n✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)\n❌ \"I've written a regression test\" (without red-green verification)\n```\n\n**Build:**\n```\n✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] \"Build passes\"\n❌ \"Linter passed\" (linter doesn't check compilation)\n```\n\n**Requirements:**\n```\n✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion\n❌ \"Tests pass, phase complete\"\n```\n\n**Agent delegation:**\n```\n✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state\n❌ Trust agent report\n```\n\n## Why This Matters\n\nFrom 24 failure memories:\n- your human partner said \"I don't believe you\" - trust broken\n- Undefined functions shipped - would crash\n- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features\n- Time wasted on false completion → redirect → rework\n- Violates: \"Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced.\"\n\n## When To Apply\n\n**ALWAYS before:**\n- ANY variation of success/completion claims\n- ANY expression of satisfaction\n- ANY positive statement about work state\n- Committing, PR creation, task completion\n- Staging, committing, pushing, or opening a PR when the work could include secrets, local environment files, dependencies, caches, build outputs, logs, temporary files, or generated/intermediate artifacts\n- Moving to next task\n- Delegating to agents\n\nWhen a meaningful bug involved architecture, storage, memory, persistence, control planes, background workers, recovery, data loss, security, or a repeated wrong assumption, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` before claiming the fix is complete.\n\nWhen a React app has server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, or persisted runtime state, use `ultrapowers:client-server-state-invariants` to decide which race and synchronization tests are required before claiming completion.\n\nBefore any git publish step, verify `.gitignore` exists for the project and that `git status` does not include secrets, credentials, local environment files, `node_modules`, dependency caches, build outputs, coverage output, logs, temporary files, or intermediate/generated artifacts unless the human explicitly asked to version a specific generated artifact.\n\n**Rule applies to:**\n- Exact phrases\n- Paraphrases and synonyms\n- Implications of success\n- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness\n\n## The Bottom Line\n\n**No shortcuts for verification.**\n\nRun the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.\n\nThis is non-negotiable.\n\n"

## Codex progress · 2026-08-01T15:26:52.215Z

This bug is a useful state-management lesson, so I’m recording it in the project’s bug notes: saving a password in a profile is not evidence that authentication succeeded, and a Connect decision must never depend only on whether the password text changed.

<!-- /codex-event -->

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"tool-call","timestamp":"2026-08-01T15:27:05.013Z","phase":null} -->
## Tool call: exec · 2026-08-01T15:27:05.013Z

```json
const patch = "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-01-connect-must-attempt-authentication.md\n+# Connect Must Attempt Authentication\n+\n+**Date:** 2026-08-01\n+**Context:** OmniHarness server profiles, saved passwords, and browser/native authorization\n+**Symptom:** Entering the server password in Edit server and pressing Connect closed the dialog but left the interface on the password screen.\n+**Root Cause:** The edit flow treated an unchanged saved password as evidence that authorization did not need to run. Native profiles therefore skipped direct login when a saved password matched the submitted value, even if their bearer credential was missing, expired, or revoked. The same-origin browser profile returned from the authorization function without using the submitted password at all. Profile persistence and authentication state had been conflated.\n+**Fix:** The current same-origin profile now submits the password through its runtime login endpoint and retries its connection. Native profile edits now authorize whenever Connect is submitted with a password, regardless of whether the saved password text changed.\n+**Verification:** `pnpm vitest run tests/ui/sidebar-layout.test.ts` was observed failing before the fix and passing afterward. Focused runner/auth tests, type checking, and the interface build were also run before completion.\n+**Prevention:** Treat Connect as an explicit authentication attempt. Decide whether to authenticate from connection and credential state plus the user's action, never from a comparison of secret text. Regression coverage must assert that the submitted password reaches a real login path and that reconnect follows successful authorization.\n+**Skill/Doc Updates:** No general skill update was needed because the existing client/server state guidance already says persisted client data cannot stand in for server-authoritative state. This project note and regression test make the authentication-specific case durable.\n*** End Patch";
text(await tools.apply_patch(patch));

```

## Codex final · 2026-08-01T15:30:17.414Z

The password was never being submitted. Connect saved the form, saw the password hadn’t changed, skipped authentication, and closed the dialog—leaving the old password screen in place.

Fixed in [RunnerControls.tsx](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/runners/RunnerControls.tsx:118):

- Current-server connections now submit the password and reconnect.
- Native connections reauthorize even when the saved password is unchanged.
- Added regression coverage, including the exact browser journey shown.

Verification passed: 92 focused tests, targeted Playwright journey, TypeScript, lint, and interface build.

Existing untracked dependency folders and lock files were left untouched.
